Evidence summary

This is not an accessibility score. The counts describe evidence states for the collected active issues. A low issue count does not mean the release is accessible; a high count does not prove a conformance failure.

32Issue, comments and every merge-request state were collected.

Likely affected

Unknown applicability

Needing verification
430Findings that still need reproduction, testing or independent review.
Findings by release applicability. Counts are evidence states, not an accessibility score.

Why this is different

A VPAT is a template; a completed report is an ACR. Most vendor ACRs are periodic, sample-based snapshots reviewed by legal or product management, with the vendor issue queue kept separate. This report is an issue-queue attestation: it is generated from the full active Drupal Core Accessibility queue and keeps every cited issue linked. It is stronger on known-report completeness, traceability and recency; it is weaker on independent page sampling and manual assistive-technology coverage.

Conventional vendor ACR and this issue-queue attestation
DimensionConventional vendor ACRThis report
CadenceAnnual, biennial or procurement-drivenEvery weekly tracker snapshot
EvidenceSelected pages, flows or a WCAG-EM sampleFull active Accessibility queue; detailed comments/MRs where collected
TraceabilitySummary claims, often without issue linksEvery cited issue links to the public Drupal.org source
AuthoringOften folded into web or omittedExplicit Authoring Tool component and separate remarks
ChangeStatic until the next reportIncremental; new issues and status changes appear next snapshot
IndependenceOften a commissioned assessmentCommunity-reported issue evidence; no independent WCAG-EM audit

This is not a WCAG-EM evaluation, a certification or a substitute for a procurement-specific ACR. A missing tagged issue is not evidence of support. Read the queue as reported barrier evidence and confirm every criterion with a reviewer.

Background: GSA OpenACR issue #180 · Drupal ACR process · WCAG-EM 2.0.

WCAG criterion evidence

Criteria are grouped from explicit Drupal.org tags such as wcag143. Tags are candidate evidence, not conformance results. Issues without a criterion tag are listed separately.

  • !397 closed → main · Issue #2158943: Replace jQuery UI dialog with supported library or polyfill

Matching commits

No matching fix commits were found in the indexed branches.

Issue summary and comments (183 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation We are in the process of deprecating jQuery UI in core. jQueryUI dialog is among the components that need to be removed/replaced. This is a long term effort going back to Drupal 9, #3067261: [Plan] Remove jQuery UI components used by Drupal core and replace with a set of supported solutions. The OpenJS Foundation lists jQuery UI as an Emeritus project in https://openjsf.org/projects/ which is described as: Emeritus projects are those which the maintainers feel have reached or are nearing end-of-life This issue was originally proposed as specifically looking for a polyfill. However, both the browser landscape and Drupal core have changed significantly since this issue was opened thirteen years ago. Today, all browsers support <dialog>. The only remaining lack is that Safari does not yet support the closedby property. In core we now have htmx, which is updated to v.4 for D12. Proposed resolution Add a web component enhanced dialog as a single directory component. Add wrapper formats to render default, modal and off-canvas dialogs for processing by HTMX using the new hx-partial custom tag. Insert dialog and side-positioned off-canvas div just before the end of the body. Insert top-positioned off-canvas div just before the main canvas wrapper so that the main content is pushed down naturally. The HtmxDialogRenderer detects a class query parameter on the request. This value is passed to the SDC and added to the dialog markup. Add an inbound path processor that takes a path /htmx/dialog/{type}/{path} and transforms it to /{path}?_wrapper_format="drupal_htmx_dialog.{type}" Remaining tasks Ready for review User interface changes This is an api level change. API changes Dynamic dialogs will now be created by a GET request to /htmx/dialog/{type}/{path} where {…

Read the full issue and discussion at Drupal.org

Merge requests

  • !16464 opened → main · Issue #2279111: consolidate and expand non-labelable label handling
  • !16441 opened → main · Do not use label elements in item render elements

Matching commits

No matching fix commits were found in the indexed branches.

Issue summary and comments (27 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation According to the HTML specification when using the "label" element the "for" attribute should only be used when the element you're labelling is "labelable". See: http://www.w3.org/html/wg/drafts/html/master/forms.html#the-label-element Labelable elements are: button input (if the type attribute is not in the Hidden state) keygen meter output progress select textarea form-associated custom elements Steps to reproduce Install Drupal with the Standard profile. Visit /admin/config/media/file-system. Inspect the markup for "Public file base URL", In addition to inspecting the markup, note the pointer as you hover over the text in Step 3. Proposed resolution Add the property non_labelable to the Item element (Drupal\Core\Render\Element\Item). Add the variable tag to all versions of form-element-label.html.twig. In core/lib/Drupal/Core/Form/FormPreprocess.php, set that variable to label if the form element is labelable (and keep the existing markup). If not, then set tag to span, add an id attribute to it, and add an aria-describedby attribute to the wrapping <div>. (Since (1) applies only to Item elements, that is the only non-labelable form element.) In future issues, we may add the non_labelable property to other form elements. In (3), the code just checks for the property, not the element type. Remaining tasks User interface changes Here is the markup for the "Public file base URL" section: see the Steps to Reproduce. Both code snippets below use the Default Admin theme. Before <div data-drupal-wrapper-selector="edit-file-public-path" id="edit-file-public-path" class="js-form-item form-item js-form-type-item form-type--item js-form-item-file-public-path form-item--file-public-path"> <label for="edit-file-public-path" class="form-item__label">Public file…

Read the full issue and discussion at Drupal.org

  1. dcam · 2014-07-08 · comment link

    Work has been done in 8.x to correct this problem. Groups of radios and checkboxes now have a legend instead of a label. Work is on-going to continue improving these elements. See the related issue. Unfortunately, this can't be fixed in core for 7.x because the change would break every theme. There may be a contributed module that will override the theming to make it accessible. I'm closing this issue as a duplicate.

However, would that really help accessibility - i.e. is pointing screen readers nowhere better than pointing them to a div? Seems like it might actually be worse...     @katannshaw and @Amber Himes Matz admittedly this is a hard problem to fix, but at the same time I don't think that it can be marked as "works as designed". To sum up the problem: <label> elements should have a for attribute that points to an HTML id of a labelable element (see https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/forms.html#the-label-element ) <div> elements are NOT labelable elements (see https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/forms.html#category-label ), so they should not be pointed to via the for attribute (and not have a <label> to begin with). So this for example, is NOT proper use o…

Merge requests

  • !7305 opened → main · support for #states for managed_file

Matching commits

No matching fix commits were found in the indexed branches.

Issue summary and comments (90 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation Cannot affect the visibility or requirement of managed_file with states. Identical to #1118016: conditional visibility of a managed_file using #states attribute does not work so either the bug wasn't fully fixed before, or a regression happened at some point and broke this functionality. Issue summary and comments (167 comments captured)

Issue summary

Follow-up to #2152217: Remove theme_form_required_marker() from the theme system - use CSS instead Updated as of #165. Problem/Motivation 3 years ago, in #2152217: Remove theme_form_required_marker() from the theme system - use CSS instead, the community decided to remove the star (*) used to indicate that a form field is required from the source code to add it from the CSS. Initially, the issue was just about converting the theme function to a Twig template but changed quickly. The purpose of this change was to simplify theming and improve accessibility. The thing is that, according to WCAG, this is not as accessible as we thought. References: WCAG 2.0 - 1.1.1 Non-text Content F3: Failure of Success Criterion 1.1.1 due to using CSS to include images that convey important information WCAG 2.0 - 1.3.1 Info and Relationships Accessiweb 2.2 - Criterion 10.2 - [Bronze] On each Web page, is visible content still available when style sheets or images are disabled? (Accessiweb is the French accessibility standard) F87: Failure of Success Criterion 1.3.1 due to inserting non-decorative content by using :before and :after pseudo-elements and the 'content' property in CSS. [The latest version of this document says that it is obsolete.] Steps to reproduce: Standard cases Go to any page including a form with required fields (eg. /user/login) Disable CSS in your browser With JavaScript states API Note: This mimics the automated test. In settings.local.php, uncomment the line $settings['extension_discovery_scan_tests'] = TRUE;, or add that line to settings.php. Enable the form_test module. Go to form-test/javascript-states-form. Check the checkbox named "Checkbox trigger". Find the textfield named "Textfield required when checkbox trigger checked", and note the required marker (star…

Issue summary and comments (112 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation The radios elements that are provided by core do not apply the required attribute to the generated <input> elements. Instead the required attribute is applied to the wrapping <fieldset>. There are two issues with this: the required state needs to be conveyed on the individual <input type="radio"> elements so that assistive technologies can correctly identify the required controls. the required attribute is not valid when applied to a <fieldset>, thus causing the page to fail W3C validation. Dynamic #states['required'] also needs to update the radio inputs. Hiding an already selected _none option does not, by itself, make a required radio group invalid. Proposed resolution Remove the required attributes from the <fieldset> of Form API radios elements. Add required attribute to the <input type=radio> elements instead. Add role="radiogroup" to the radios fieldset. CSS updates, to ensure no visual regressions. For dynamic required states, update the radio inputs and required indicator; hide or show _none without changing the selection, and require a real choice when _none remains selected. Remaining tasks DONE. Move required attributes from the <fieldset> element to the <input> element. DONE. Add the required="required" attribute to the <input type="radio"> elements. DONE. CSS to avoid visual regression. Asterisk should be after the fieldset legend, not the individual radio buttons. DONE. Update dynamic #states['required'] handling for radio groups, including updating the radio inputs and required indicator. DONE. Preserve the existing checked state of the _none option when the required state toggles; hide/show the option without automatically selecting or clearing it, while ensuring a required group with _none selected remains invalid. Manual testing ti…

Issue summary and comments (80 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation The AJAX API has a feature to display a progress message, but it isn't conveyed to assistive tech like screen readers. '#ajax' => array( 'callback' => 'Drupal\config_translation\FormElement\DateFormat::ajaxSample', 'event' => 'keyup', 'progress' => array( 'type' => 'throbber', 'message' => t('Loading more products'), ), ), Proposed resolution Find a way to convey the progress message. Possibilities (use one approach only, not both): Mark the visible AJAX message as an ARIA-live region (preferred?), or Duplicate the visible message using a Drupal.announce() call. Other possibilities might be to provide incrementally updated messages, like "Updating... 30%... 80%... finished". Remaining tasks Perform manual testing Perform accessibility review User interface changes No visual changes. Format the AJAX message so it is conveyed to assistive technology, e.g. so screen readers can announce it. API changes Several screenreader related properties have been added to the AJAX Progress settings. announce - Determines if screenreaders should announce progress for this operation announceDelay - The delay in milliseconds before the screenreader announces the progress announceIntervalTime - The interval in milliseconds between each announcement announceMessage - The message to be announced by the screenreader, if no message property is set. Data model changes No data model changes introduced. Background reading How do you make the Loading icon accessible for screen-readers like JAWS? Accessibility: Page Loader indicator using aria-live JavaScript and Accessibility: Don't Blame the Language (DrupalCon Nashville 2018 session). Recording and slides from Everett Zufelt's talk. JavaScript and Accessibility: Don’t Blame the Language, Everett Zufelt's article version on M…

  • !3037 opened → main · Issue #2983456: Expose triggering update of media metadata + thumbnail to end users

Matching commits

No matching fix commits were found in the indexed branches.

Issue summary and comments (175 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation It's currently impossible for end-users to manually pull metadata associated with media assets from the source into the Drupal media entity, after the entity is created. Similarly, the Thumbnail (which is not a metadata per se) is treated the same way, it is currently only retrieved from the source when the entity is first created. Several scenarios have shown this is a big limitation, being a prominent example the situation when the source providing the thumbnail is remote (like an YouTube video), and the thumbnail is changed on the remote source after the entity has been created in Drupal. Currently there is no mechanism for users to update the Thumbnail in this scenario. The same is valid for all other metadata attributes that source plugins could define (name, filesize, filemime, width, height, etc). This issue was spun-off from #2878119: Whether queued or not, update the media thumbnail and metadata before beginning the entity save database transaction. Proposed resolution Expose to the UI the possibility of end users with correct permissions to trigger an immediate update of the asset's metadata + thumbnail Remaining tasks Usability re-review, see #108 and the last paragraph of #138 #67, testing with multiple languages #80.2 - a security item #138 1-3. Usability review in #166 User interface changes - New contextual link "Update metadata" is available in the front-end - New operation dropbutton "Update metadata" is available in the back-end - New "Update metadata" action is available in media administrative views, as a bulk operation. API changes A new public method \Drupal\media\MediaInterface::updateMetadata() is added to the Media interface. Data model changes None Release notes snippet Adds the ability for users to manually trigger updating…

Read the full issue and discussion at Drupal.org

Supporting disabled buttons leads to poor usability and accessibility

Drupal.org issue #3049086 · drupal:3049086

Candidate WCAG criteria

Matching commits

No matching fix commits were found in the indexed branches.

Issue summary and comments (8 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation There's research to prove that using disabled button elements is bad for usability and accessibility. For example, Disabled buttons suck provides a comprehensive explanation of why disabled buttons shouldn't be used, and what should be used to replace them. The core problem is that when a button is disabled, the user won't know why it is disabled. Preventing actions could be still done by either not showing the button at all, or by showing the button as active, and after a user clicks a "disabled" button, the system would show an error including an explanation of why the action couldn't be triggered. At the moment, we support the disabled state by specifically adding classes to buttons based on disabled state. We also provide styles for the disabled state in our themes. Proposed resolution Deprecate disabled state for buttons in Drupal 8.8.0 and explain that all explicit support for them will be removed in Drupal 9. Since we don't want to be on the way of people, people should be still allowed to pass in the attribute buttons but it wouldn't be supported by Drupal themes out of the box. This way people would be still able to achieve the same end result in custom projects, but we discourage contrib projects and core from using this pattern. Remaining tasks User interface changes API changes Data model changes Release notes snippet

Read the full issue and discussion at Drupal.org

Selected comments

  1. andrewmacpherson · 2019-04-18 · comment link

    I'm very skeptical of this. Whatever the problems with disabled buttons (especially styling), they are part of the HTML rec, and I don't think we can ignore that. Developers WILL create disabled buttons in their custom modules, and if core themes don't support this, users will have no way to know that the button is disabled, and that will be the fault of our core themes. There's an interesting response to the axesslab article here - https://medium.com/@DanielKoster/disabled-buttons-dont-have-to-suck-6dcd...

Both these suggestions can work, but they depend on the context of the application. These might be interesting to create as separate FAPI element types, with their attached behaviours. Since we don't want to be on the way of people, people should be still allowed to pass in the attribute buttons but it wouldn't be supported by Drupal theme…

  3. lauriii · 2019-04-18 · comment link

    The problem that I tried to convey is that from our documentation, and API point of view, there's no risk at all in marking buttons disabled. There's explicit support for that. Also because we are lacking metadata, it's not possible to fix this in theme level. AFAIK, there aren't any excuses for marking button disabled without providing an explanation of why the button has been disabled. If an explanation isn't provided, there's always room a chance that some users get confused.     It's been 3 years, so here are some updated references (via AI): MDN: aria-disabled, updated November 2025. Explains the difference between native disabled and aria-disabled. MDN: inert. Relevant when an entire region, rather than one form control, must be temporarily unavailable. Inert content is removed from both the tab order and accessibility tree, so it is not interchangeable with disabled. WHATWG HTML: disabled form controls. This should be the normative HTML reference. WAI-ARIA 1.2: aria-disabled. This defines the programmatic state, but does not supply behaviour. GOV.UK Design System:…

Language switcher block is an unlabelled navigation landmark region

Merge requests

  • !16323 opened → main · Language switcher block is an unlabelled navigation landmark region

Matching commits

No matching fix commits were found in the indexed branches.

Issue summary and comments (13 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation The language switcher block has a navigation landmark region navigation role, but the region has no accessible name. An accessible name is not strictly needed for all landmark regions. However in situations where there are multiple landmark regions of the same type on the page, the best practice is to differentiate these with an accessible name. We already do this for our menu and breadcrumb blocks. Proposed resolution Add an aria-labelledby reference to the language switcher navigation landmark region, pointing to the nested visually-hidden heading. Follow the same pattern as we already use for menu blocks. Remaining tasks patch User interface changes Adds an accessible name to the language switcher navigation landmark region, so screen reader users can differentiate it from other navigation landmarks on the page. API changes None. Data model changes None.

Read the full issue and discussion at Drupal.org

Issue summary and comments (61 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation #3553673: Migrate from Nightwatch to Playwright (and phpunit Axe) tests will remove Nightwatch Axe tests from core. This issue is to set up Axe testing in PHPUnit and migrate the tests. Proposed resolution Create a PHP trait to inject Axe-core for tests and run it on the page. Create a new base class for FunctionalJavascript tests that uses the trait and defines a test runner which developers can leverage by simply creating a data provider. Update .gitlab-ci.yml to run yarn install before FunctionalJavascript tests. Proposed error message format <BASE_URL> will be replaced with the actual test site URL. Accessibility test failures (2 total) 1. [serious] Elements must meet minimum color contrast ratio thresholds Test URL: <BASE_URL>/accessibility-test Axe rule: `color-contrast` Violating targets (1): * `#target-low-contrast` 2. [critical] Form elements must have labels Test URL: <BASE_URL>/accessibility-test Axe rule: `label` Violating targets (1): * `input` Remaining tasks Resolve issue with javascript dependency. Update tests for any new cases that have been added. Remove existing Nightwatch a11y tests. Copy recent changes in the Navigation module Nightwatch a11y test to the corresponding AccessibilityTest.php in this MR. Check if pipeline for rebase in #47 passes. Fix remaining test failures Decide on MVP for error messages. Decide what, if anything, to do with incomplete results. They are not currently included in the output, as axe-core separates them from violations. User interface changes Introduced terminology API changes Data model changes Release notes snippet

Read the full issue and discussion at Drupal.org

All that to say that I think the only advantage of this one instead of Nightwatch's one would be that some devs could be more comfortable using PHPUnit than Nigthwatch. Merge requests

  • !5245 opened → main · Update pager ellipsis aria attributes

Matching commits

No matching fix commits were found in the indexed branches.

Issue summary and comments (82 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation When testing Olivero's pager (and displaying enough page numbers to get the ellipsis to appear), deque axe-core reports this issue <ul> and <ol> must only directly contain <li>, <script> or <template> elements Ensures that lists are structured correctly more informationLink opens in a new window Element Location: .pager__items <ul class="pager__items js-pager__items"> To solve this problem, you need to fix the following: List element has direct children that are not allowed: [role=presentation] Related Node <li class="pager__item pager__item--ellipsis" role="presentation">…</li> Steps to reproduce Create a view that uses a full pager, and a sufficient amount of content to create many pages for that view. Proposed resolution Replace role="presentation" with aria-label="additional pages" similar to the USWDS https://github.com/uswds/uswds/issues/5022#issuecomment-1476832757 Remove role="presentation" and provide visually hidden text inside the element: <li> <span aria-hidden="true">…</span> <span class="visually-hidden">Ellipsis indicating non-visible pages</span> </li> Remaining tasks User interface changes None API changes None Data model changes None Release notes snippet None

Read the full issue and discussion at Drupal.org

Selected comments

  1. shaal · 2023-03-16 · comment link

  2. shaal · 2023-03-16 · comment link

  3. gauravvvv · 2023-03-17 · comment link

    I have removed the role="presentation" and added the aria-label="additional pages". please review

  4. idebr · 2023-03-17 · comment link

  5. smustgrave · 2023-03-17 · comment link

    This affects all themes. Since it covers all it should have a test.

  6. smustgrave · 2023-03-17 · comment link

    Closed #3347326: Axe error reported on role=presentation in pagination as a duplicate

  7. mgifford · 2023-03-18 · comment link

    Thanks for closing that duplicate. This is also being discussed here with the USWDS: https://github.com/uswds/uswds/issues/5022

  8. chi · 2023-03-18 · comment link

    - <li class="pager__item pager__item--ellipsis" role="presentation">&hellip;</li> + <li class="pager__item pager__item--ellipsis" aria-label="additional pages">&hellip;</li> "additional pages" needs to be translated. AFAIK aria-label does not make sense on non-interactive elements. Most of screen readers will simply ignore it. I think aria-hidden attribute would be more suitable here.

Merge requests

  • !8985 opened → main · Issue #3459246: Add a '#selectall' option for checkboxes element
  • !8678 opened → main · Issue #3459246: Add a '#selectall' option for checkboxes element

Matching commits

No matching fix commits were found in the indexed branches.

Issue summary and comments (81 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation With the Checkboxes element Drupal provides a super helpful form element to show a list of checkboxes. For more complex cases, especially with several columns, there's Tableselect. Creating forms with many options in the Checkboxes Element leads to bad UX, if you have to select all checkboxes one by one. Instead, the Drupal Elements should provide good UX and DX out of the box and that way also ensure consistency. For that reason, I'd like to propose introducing a '#checkall' => TRUE / FALSE option for Checkboxes. Usage example: $form['favorites']['colors'] = [ '#type' => 'checkboxes', '#options' => ['blue' => $this->t('Blue'), 'red' => $this->t('Red')], '#check_all' => TRUE, // This makes the "Check all" functionality appear! '#title' => $this->t('Which colors do you like?'), ... ]; (https://api.drupal.org/api/drupal/core%21lib%21Drupal%21Core%21Render%21...) There have been several requests for that in the past, see below, so I think it makes sense. Initially, I created my request for the "(Un)check all" feature in a field_tools issue, but @joachim correctly pointed out that it would make more sense to have this functionality generally available in Drupal Core Checkboxes element: It would be useful, but I'd rather see that implemented in the core checkboxes element that in this module. Also see the positive feedback by Chris Matthews in #19: Personally +1 for bringing multiple_select type functionality generally into core. Basically standard UX on the web, but up to the UX team/maintainers to decide. Helpful comments in #3 from 12 years ago I'd be in support of expanding #type=checkboxes to allow this property, as we do with #type=tableselect. Other than the permissions page (where I assume you would be suggesting a select all at the top of each ro…

Read the full issue and discussion at Drupal.org

Issue summary and comments (112 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation After SDC in Drupal 10.1 (July 2023), the Icon API in Drupal 11.1 (Dec 2024) and the Design Tokens API in Drupal 11.5 (Dec 2026), let’s continue to implement design systems API in Core, in order to be able to build business agnostic, shareable, Drupal themes, providing design implementations which can be leveraged by display building tools. We are proposing a Style API to allow UI implementation owners (aka Drupal themers) to declare both: style utilities & helpers: single purpose, universal, local UI alterations themes & modes: multi purpose, not always universal, UI alterations 1. Styles utilities & helpers Styles utilities are a common artifact of design systems. Examples: Bootstrap’s utilities: https://getbootstrap.com/docs/5.3/utilities/api/ Tailwind in DaisyUI: https://daisyui.com/docs/utilities/ Material’s styles: https://m3.material.io/styles Bulma’s helpers: https://bulma.io/documentation/helpers PatternFly’s utility classes : https://www.patternfly.org/utility-classes/about-utility-classes USWDS’s utilities: https://designsystem.digital.gov/utilities/ ... Each style is a set of mutually exclusive, self-descriptive, single-purpose, universal, CSS classes. Examples: Typography, orders, Colors, Spacing, Elevation.... Analysis of the current solutions in the contrib space Like SDC and Icon API, we believe it must be front-dev friendly, UI logic focused, YAML plugin declaration available in Drupal theme. So, let's have a look on contrib modules are already covering this scope. UI Styles (usage: 2500) https://www.drupal.org/project/ui_styles Discovery: {provider}.ui_styles.yml in modules and themes. Minimal example (Bootstrap 5 background color utility): background_color: label: Background color options: bg-primary: Primary bg-secondary: Secondar…

Issue summary and comments (9 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation The SEARCH (<search>) HTML element or search aria role (role="search") is: > used to identify the search functionality; the section of the page used to search the page, site, or collection of sites. See: - https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Reference/Elements/search - https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/Accessibility/ARIA/Referenc... Steps to reproduce Proposed resolution Use . HTML (including search) is always better, if possible, than using ARIA. #8 Remaining tasks User interface changes Introduced terminology API changes Data model changes Release notes snippet

Selected comments

  1. vitaliyb98 · 2025-06-18 · comment link

    Added the search [ARIA] role to the search form provided by the core Search module to improve accessibility and semantic markup: core/modules/search/src/Form/SearchBlockForm.php and core/modules/search/src/Form/SearchPageForm.php

  2. smustgrave · 2025-06-18 · comment link

    For good practice issue summary should be complete. May need accessibility sign off

  3. joachim · 2025-06-18 · comment link

    > Added the search [ARIA] role to the search form provided by the core Search module to improve accessibility and semantic markup: That looks useful too, but the issue was about Views. Also, an a11y problem is a bug.

  4. vitaliyb98 · 2025-06-20 · comment link

    Hi @joachim, could you please provide more details? Do you mean adding this attribute to every Exposed Form, even if it doesn't contain a text input for example, if it only has checkboxes?

  5. mgifford · 2025-07-07 · comment link

    I made some small changes to the issue summary. The latest MR adds the ARIA search role to the existing form. It would be better though to switch core/modules/search/src/Form/SearchBlockForm.php & core/modules/search/src/Form/SearchPageForm.php to just use <search> though. HTML (including search) is always better, if possible, than using ARIA.

  6. vitaliyb98 · 2025-08-18 · comment link

    I tried to wrap the search form with the HTML element using a template override. But adding extra markup around the form broke the styles and JavaScript for the search block (tested in the Olivero theme). For now, it seems we can only safely add this wrapper directly in the Olivero theme, together with fixes for the broken search functionality. If we add the wrapper globally (for example, in the Search module), it could break other themes as well.

  7. quietone · 2026-10-31 · comment link

    Work done by longwave using an LLM that identified this as a possible duplicate of #3410702: Use the new search html element in place of aria-role=search. I closed that one and updated the issue summary here with the recommended solution by @mgifford

Merge requests

  • !16520 opened → main · Use Attribute ID
  • !13771 closed → main · Issue #3557245 by gease: Use $form_element['#attributes']['id] instead of...

Matching commits

No matching fix commits were found in the indexed branches.

Issue summary and comments (24 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation The links to the form elements with errors are (sometimes) wrong, they use non-existent anchors (ids on the page) Steps to reproduce Install the Standard profile. Enable the inline_form_errors module. Create a content type. Add a required file upload field of type 'image' to the content type. Create new content of the content type To limit the validation errors give the node a title. Submit the form without uploading an image so that it doesn't pass validation for the required field. Before The inline form error link will have a fragment like #edit-field-image-0 (depending on what you named the field). But the form element that has the error has an ID like #edit-field-image-0-upload. The link does not have a valid target. Proposed resolution \Drupal\Core\Theme\ThemeManager::render() uses $element['#attributes']['id'] to send as an element id to the template if (isset($info['render element'])) { $key = $info['render element']; if (isset($variables[$key]['#attributes'])) { $variables['attributes'] = AttributeHelper::mergeCollections($variables['attributes'], $variables[$key]['#attributes']); } } (at least for fieldset elements), while \Drupal\inline_form_errors\FormErrorHandler::displayErrorMessages() uses $form_element['#id'] to build the link. And they are not the same. So we should use $form_element['#attributes']['id'] instead of $form_element['#id'] in \Drupal\inline_form_errors\FormErrorHandler::displayErrorMessages(). Remaining tasks Code review User interface changes N/A Introduced terminology N/A API changes N/A Data model changes N/A Release notes snippet N/A

Read the full issue and discussion at Drupal.org

  • !16335 opened → main · fixing accessibility issue 3587661 related to file widget display checkbox labels

Matching commits

No matching fix commits were found in the indexed branches.

Issue summary and comments (17 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation This accessibility report highlights a violation of WCAG 4.1.2 (Name, Role, Value) involving a checkbox within a Drupal file widget, specifically the element #edit-imagefile-file-limited-0-display. The issue is that the input field lacks a programmatically associated name, which prevents screen readers from conveying the purpose of the checkbox to users. To resolve this, the developer must provide an accessible label by either wrapping the input in a tag, using the for attribute to link an explicit label, or implementing ARIA attributes like aria-label or aria-labelledby. Ensuring this element is properly labeled is critical for making the form navigable and understandable for individuals using assistive technologies. Steps to reproduce Standard install, logged in as an administrator. Add a File field to Article, with "Allowed number of values" set to 2 or Unlimited, and "Enable Display field" ticked. Upload a .txt file to that field at /node/add/article. Check the checkbox in the files table's "Display" column: it has no label Proposed resolution Add aria label to the file widget.   • !16432 opened → main · Implement dark-mode (color-scheme) settings per user

Matching commits

No matching fix commits were found in the indexed branches.

Issue summary and comments (113 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation Drupal currently lacks a standardized way to store and manage individual user interface preferences. This issue establishes the foundation for per-user UI preferences, starting with color scheme (light, dark, or system). Preferences will be stored as base fields on the user entity, similar to the existing timezone and language preferences, and configured through the user profile form. Future issues may introduce additional preferences, including: UI density: Adjusting the spacing and compactness of interface elements. Sticky form actions: Keeping form action buttons visible at the top of the page. Other user-specific interface preferences. Motivation for color scheme Light/dark mode is currently owned entirely by the new Default Admin theme. The preference lives in that theme's settings, so it only applies where that theme is rendering. Anywhere else - the front end, the Navigation toolbar and top bar shown to a logged-in user while browsing the front end, or a site still on Claro - the user's choice is simply not respected. The practical effect is a site that flips appearance as the user moves around it: a light Navigation toolbar on the front end, then a dark admin screen one click later. That is jarring, and for a premium CMS it reads as sloppy. It is also more than cosmetic. Some people genuinely cannot work comfortably in one scheme or the other, so being able to choose - and having that choice honored consistently - is an accessibility concern, even where no specific WCAG success criterion applies. Every browser and operating system already exposes a color-scheme preference and users expect applications to follow it. Today there is no place in Drupal for "this user prefers light, dark, or whatever the system says" to live. Nothing outside one t…

Read the full issue and discussion at Drupal.org

Merge requests

  • !17335 opened → main · fix: #3611425 Navigation admin "Extend" button has hidden text before menu title

Matching commits

No matching fix commits were found in the indexed branches.

Issue summary and comments (4 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem WCAG 2.5.3 Label in Name requires that the accessible name of a control contains the visible label text. The navigation admin menu "Extend" button has visually-hidden text that precedes the visible menu item title. Visible text: Menu item title (e.g., "Content", "Structure") Accessible name: "Extend Content", "Extend Structure" A speech recognition user who says "Click Content" may not match because the accessible name starts with "Extend", not "Content". Proposed resolution Move the visually-hidden "Extend" text after the menu item title: &lt;button class="toolbar-menu__link toolbar-menu__link--{{ menu_level }}" data-toolbar-menu-trigger="{{ menu_level }}" aria-expanded="false" data-index-text="{{ item.title|first|lower }}" &gt; &lt;span class="toolbar-menu__link-title"&gt;{{ item.title }}&lt;/span&gt; &lt;span data-toolbar-action class="toolbar-menu__link-action visually-hidden"&gt;{{ 'Extend'|t }}&lt;/span&gt; {{ icon('navigation', 'chevron', { class: 'toolbar-menu__chevron', size: 16 }) }} &lt;/button&gt; File core/modules/navigation/templates/navigation-menu.html.twig — lines 138-139 Test Add a FunctionalJavascript test that: Visits the admin navigation Asserts each expandable menu button's accessible name starts with the visible menu item title Related Meta: Assess Drupal core for WCAG 2.5.3 Label in Name WCAG 2.5.3 Label in Name

Read the full issue and discussion at Drupal.org

  • !16609 opened → main · Issue #3615469: Move 'page' inside the user login link on the maintenance mode form

Matching commits

No matching fix commits were found in the indexed branches.

Issue summary and comments (15 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation On the maintenance mode settings form (/admin/config/development/maintenance), the description of the "Put site into maintenance mode" checkbox ends with: Authorized users can log in directly via the user login page. where the link wraps only the words "user login", leaving " page." outside the anchor. The string lives in core/modules/system/src/Form/SiteMaintenanceModeForm.php (line 89 in 11.x). Two accessibility problems follow: Link purpose (WCAG 2.4.4). Read from a screen reader's links list or rotor, the link text is the partial phrase "user login": the object of the sentence is "user login page", and "page" is stranded outside the link. "User login" on its own is not a meaningful link target the way "user login page" is (see #11). The "permission" link earlier in the same sentence has the same shape. This is cross-platform and independent of any screen reader quirk. Run-together announcement on iOS VoiceOver. The description is associated with the checkbox via aria-describedby. When iOS VoiceOver reads the computed description, it drops the whitespace at the link boundary, so the description is announced as "... via the user loginpage." Retesting narrowed this to iOS specifically (see #10): macOS VoiceOver with Safari and Chrome reads the phrase correctly, and desktop Chromium's computed accessible description contains the space. It only reproduces on a real iOS device. An earlier version of this summary called it cross-engine; that was wrong, since on an iPhone every browser is WebKit under the same VoiceOver layer. Device testing of both candidate wordings (see #12) showed that either the MR's wording or the Interface Text Standards quoted style fixes the iOS announcement, so the announcement is no longer the deciding factor. What remains is…

Issue summary and comments (40 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation Inline Form Errors replaces the error messages in the summary block at the top of the page with links to the invalid fields. For errors inside details and vertical tabs, clicking the error link opens the details / vertical tab and focuses the invalid field. For errors in Default Admin's closed sidebar, clicking the error link does nothing. The sidebar remains closed, and the invalid field remains hidden. I think this causes failure of WCAG SC 3.3.3 Error Suggestion (Level AA). Inline Form Errors moves the descriptive error messages with suggestions to the invalid fields. If users can't find the invalid fields, they also won't find the detailed error messages. Related: The closed sidebar provides no indication that it contains invalid fields. Working on that in #3619387: Indicate on forms sidebar toggle button that sidebar has child element errors. Steps to reproduce Set Default Admin as the admin theme. Enable the Inline Form Errors module. Go to /node/add/article. Reduce viewport width until the sidebar on the right containing the "advanced" fields collapses. Click the toggle button to expand the sidebar. Enter a value without a beginning slash in the URL alias field. Close the sidebar. Click the Save button. This should cause a validation error at the top of the page, something like the following with "URL alias" being a link: "1 error has been found: URL alias". Click the "URL alias" link in the error message. Expected The sidebar is opened. Focus moves to the invalid URL alias field. Actual Nothing happens when the "URL alias" link is clicked.   • !16911 opened → main · Sidebar toggle fixes

Matching commits

No matching fix commits were found in the indexed branches.

Issue summary and comments (29 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation From #3618906: [Meta] Forms sidebar accessibility issues The toggle control shouldn't be rendered as a link with role="button". If it's meant to function as a link (IOW, it's supposed to go somewhere usable) without JavaScript: Render it as a standard link. Either change it to a button or add role="button" in the JavaScript when the toggle behavior is added. If it's not meant to function as a link without JavaScript, render it as a button. The toggle control should be operable with the SPACE key, probably on keyup. See https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/disclosure/examples/disclosure-..., https://adrianroselli.com/2022/04/brief-note-on-buttons-enter-and-space..... The accessible name (& probably the title attribute) of the toggle control probably shouldn't change according to the state. See #3093378: Use ARIA disclosure pattern for submenu buttons in vertical toolbar orientation for @andrewmacphereson's explanation of why. Similar to the toggle control, the close button shouldn't be rendered as a link with role="button". I can't imagine that is supposed to function as a link in any circumstance, so it should probably just be a button element. Probably not really an accessibility issue, now that I think about it. Just bad practice. Proposed resolution Changes to the entity edit form sidebar in default_admin: Splits migration/js/sidebar.js into a sidebar-toggle SDC and js/edit-sidebar.js. Toggle and close controls are now <button> elements. Sets the open/closed state before first paint. The sidebar no longer flashes open. Opening the sidebar no longer collapses Navigation when page content changes height. Below 1024px, content under the overlay is inert. Focus moves into the sidebar, and Escape closes it. Positions the sidebar with CSS variables.…

  • !17211 opened → main · fix: #3624877 navigation icons contain hardcoded colors

Matching commits

No matching fix commits were found in the indexed branches.

Issue summary and comments (10 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation There are a number of icons in the navigation module that contain hard-coded color values, which makes them behave poorly in dark mode or whenever users are attempting to implement them in non-standard contexts. Update: In reference to my later comment in #7, the icons work fine in light or dark mode so long as they are given a magic CSS class at render time. If that class is omitted, however, as in the case of any custom implementation, the icons do no respect currentColor conventions. Steps to reproduce After a quick search of the navigation module codebase, I've determined that the following svg files have hard-coded colors that could stand to be changed to "currentColor". However, we should be absolutely sure that we've done some testing before just changing them; I imagine that some icons may have a two-tone color palette on purpose to communicate a certain message. radioactive.svg* signpost.svg warning.svg error.svg database.svg file.svg * the radioactive icon is stored in the tests directory of navigation. Do we need to change this file or would it cause a testing regression? Proposed resolution Change hardcoded values to currentColor Remaining tasks Screenshots #7 radioactive-after-dark.png 2.25 KB abenbow #7 radioactive-after-light.png 2.26 KB abenbow #7 radioactive-before-dark.png 2.26 KB abenbow #7 radioactive-before-light.png 2.21 KB abenbow #7 icons-dark-after.png 6.55 KB abenbow Review User interface changes Introduced terminology API changes Data model changes Release notes snippet

  • !17421 opened → 11.4.x · fix: #3630425 Multi-value field widget table renders an empty th for the actions column

Matching commits

No matching fix commits were found in the indexed branches.

Issue summary and comments (9 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation Since Drupal 10.2 (#1038316), the table rendered for multi-value field widgets has a column for the per-item "Remove" button. The header cell for that column is an empty array, so it renders as an empty &lt;th&gt;&lt;/th&gt;. In 11.x this is in \Drupal\Core\Field\FieldPreprocess::preprocessFieldMultipleValueForm() (previously template_preprocess_field_multiple_value_form()): $header = [ [ 'data' => [ /* h4 field label */ ], 'colspan' => 2, 'class' => ['field-label'], ], [], $this->t('Order', [], ['context' => 'Sort order']), ]; Accessibility checkers report an empty table header on every form with an unlimited/multi-value field: axe-core's empty-table-header rule and Editoria11y's "Empty table header" check. Screen reader users get no name for the column that holds the Remove buttons. This relates to WCAG 1.3.1 Info and Relationships. #3587682 reported an empty table header from an automated scan, but it was closed as "cannot reproduce". That report pointed at a different page. This issue is the reproducible case in the field widget table. Steps to reproduce Install Drupal with the Standard profile (Claro as the admin theme). Add a reference field (paragraph reference) with unlimited cardinality to the Article content type. Go to /node/add/article. Inspect the field's table header: the third &lt;th&gt; is empty. Run axe DevTools or Editoria11y on the page: it reports an empty table header. Proposed resolution Give the actions column header a visually hidden label so it has an accessible name without changing the visual design: [ 'data' => [ '#type' => 'html_tag', '#tag' => 'span', '#value' => $this->t('Operations'), '#attributes' => ['class' => ['visually-hidden']], ], ], "Operations" matches the label core already uses for action columns elsewhere,…

Read the full issue and discussion at Drupal.org

It now holds a visually hidden "Operations" label, so the column has an accessible name and nothing changes visually. Claro's preprocess_field_multiple_value_form still works: for disabled widgets, its loop adds the is-disabled class to header cells that have a data key, so this cell gets it like the others. Merge requests

  • !17617 opened → main · fix: #3633792 Prevent AJAX dialogs from jumping after opening

Matching commits

No matching fix commits were found in the indexed branches.

Issue summary and comments (13 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation While investigating #3631339: [random test failure] FieldTest::testModalDialogTitle fails intermittently, I found that Drupal dialogs first open at the wrong position, which is then corrected about 20ms later: This fitted surprisingly well with a subconscious aversion to Drupal dialogs that I'd had for some time. Like an unexplainable, involuntary, autonomic reaction, something just felt off. Now I can explain it, managed to capture the quite perceptible jump in a video, and we may now fix its cause. While not reaching the three flashes threshold of WCAG 2.3.1/2.3.2, the abrupt repositioning is still distracting and may cause averse reactions in other users as well. Unnecessarily abrupt visual motion may even contribute to dizziness, nausea or headaches in motion-sensitive users, as described in Understanding SC 2.3.3. Steps to reproduce While not necessarily restricted to Views, to reproduce the issue please: Edit some view in Views UI. Scroll down and open some dialog. Notice (or film in slow-motion) the dialog first appearing too far down, then jumping upwards. Proposed resolution To remove the delayed initial resize, we need to calculate the dialog's size and position correctly before opening the dialog. Getting there however requires a few related bugfixes: Normalize numeric strings with or without "px" to numbers, and convert percentage heights to pixels. Normalize autoResize to a boolean after beforecreate listeners had a chance to modify it. Set position: fixed for dialogs with autoResize enabled before jQuery UI calculates the initial coordinates. Preserve positioning options that keep non-modal dialogs within the viewport. Retain autoResize update on viewport and displacement changes using the shared calculation methods. To avoid regression…

Read the full issue and discussion at Drupal.org

Merge requests

  • !17698 opened → main · fix: #3635418 Inline form error summary link is broken on textarea/textfield widgets

Matching commits

No matching fix commits were found in the indexed branches.

Issue summary and comments (4 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation When inline_form_errors is enabled, there is a summary that is displayed at the top of the page in a status message, when the form has field errors. It is supposed to contain anchor links to the relevant fields, for the user to click on and correct the error. However, on the text_textarea and text_textfield widgets, the anchor link is broken. It links to #edit-foobar-0, which is an ID that doesn't exist on the page, instead of #edit-foobar-0-value, which is the actual form field element. The root cause is that the errorElement function on those widgets doesn't point to the proper form value element. Steps to reproduce 1. Ensure that inline_form_errors is enabled. 2. Create a text or text_long field to a node bundle. 3. Add a constraint to the field definition on the bundle. 4. Create and save a node with a value that triggers a constraint validation error on the field. 5. Note that the link in the error summary status message does not work. Proposed resolution Update the errorElement function to point to value.

Read the full issue and discussion at Drupal.org

Issue summary and comments (7 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation I've opened that issue already over in the Gin queue: https://git.drupalcode.org/project/gin/-/work_items/3506311. Over the course of the last few days I was able to slowly catch up again after being out of the loop for the last few months except for my attendance in the weekly ux meetings. The styling of primary and secondary local tasks in the Default Admin Theme has two main problems: Color contrast wise, it is not meeting WCAG2.2 SC1.4.11 You have shapeshifting objects, which means the same objects, tabs in this case, that are looking completely different. Active primary tabs have a chip or button-like styling, while the non-active tabs are not visually connected; they are just connected by proximity. The secondary tab has a familiar design for tabs with a strong bottom border for the active secondary tab and a barely visible light thin bottom border connecting all the available secondary tabs. That way the overall cognitive load is high: A listing for the active primary local task, comparing the chip color against its background: Blue: light #dae3ff #fafaff 1.23:1 🚫 dark #353640 #1b1b1d 1.44:1 🚫 Light Blue light #dde4f7 #fafaff 1.22:1 🚫 dark #33383f #1b1b1d 1.46:1 🚫 Dark Purple light #e0d3f5 #faf8fd 1.35:1 🚫 dark #3e3440 #1b1b1d 1.44:1 🚫 Purple light #e5d2ff #fcf8ff 1.33:1 🚫 dark #3e3140 #1b1b1d 1.41:1 🚫 Teal light #d9e8e6 #f9fbfb 1.22:1 🚫 dark #2e3b3b #1b1b1d 1.48:1 🚫 Green light #d8e7d9 #f9fbf8 1.23:1 🚫 dark #2f3a32 #1b1b1d 1.45:1 🚫 Pink light #fadee5 #fffafa 1.22:1 🚫 dark #3f3139 #1b1b1d 1.41:1 🚫 Red light #fbd9db #fff9f8 1.25:1 🚫 dark #3f3131 #1b1b1d 1.39:1 🚫 Orange light #f7e1d9 #fffaf8 1.21:1 🚫 dark #3f332e #1b1b1d 1.42:1 🚫 Yellow light #eee3d6 #fdfaf8 1.22:1 🚫 dark #3f372c #1b1b1d 1.46:1 🚫 Neutral light #d5d5d5 #f7f7f7 1.37:1 🚫 dark #3838…

Steps to reproduce Go to admin/appearance/settings/default_admin and try to figure out which of the accent color circles is the active one. Issue summary and comments (3 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation I've opened that issue already over in the Gin queue: https://git.drupalcode.org/project/gin/-/work_items/3506312 There are two problems:. First the styling for the switch component differs between light and dark mode. In light mode you have the same gray background for every accent color in the off state and the same green color in the on state. While in dark mode you are having the same gray background color for every accent color in the off state while in the on state the background color mirrors the chosen accent color. In light mode the color contrast between the switch background and the page background is high enough, same for the color contrast between the switch background and the white knob. Due to the fact that in dark mode the accent color is used for the on state a lot of combinations fail SC1.4.11. I've labeled the contrasts that dont meet SC1.4.11 in the following list with 🚫. Off State (the same for every accent color choice): page #3b3b3f #2a2a2d 1.28:1 🚫 knob #3b3b3f #ffffff 11.15:1 ✅ => the switch looks more like a checkbox, in particular with several switch toggles right underneath eachother, because the background has a way too low contrast against the page background. On State: Blue page #555aa3 #2a2a2d 2.1:1 🚫 knob #555aa3 #ffffff 6.22:1 ✅ Light Blue: page #50608d #2a2a2d 2.32:1 🚫 knob #50608d #ffffff 6.17:1 ✅ Dark Purple page #6a4b9a #2a2a2d 2.1:1 🚫 knob #6a4b9a #ffffff 6.81:1 ✅ Purple page #7049ae #2a2a2d 2.22:1 🚫 knob #7049ae #ffffff 6.48:1 ✅ Teal page #436663 #2a2a2d 2.27:1 🚫 knob #436663 #ffffff 6.33:1 ✅ Green page #436746 #2a2a2d 2.23:1 🚫 knob #436746 #ffffff 6.41:1 ✅ Pink page #50608d #2a2a2d 2.27:1 🚫 knob #50608d #ffffff 6.29:1 ✅ Red page #974743 #2a2a2d 2.25:1 🚫 knob #974743 #ffffff 6.39:1 ✅ Orange page #88533e #2a2a2d…

Read the full issue and discussion at Drupal.org

Issue summary and comments (2 comments captured)

Issue summary

Problem/Motivation There is a regression from[#3506321]. The toggle button in the lower left corner of the screen is using --admin-toolbar-color-gray-300 in light and in dark mode. light mode #b8c1d0/#2a2a2d 1.81:1 🚫 dark mode #b8c1d0/#2a2a2d 7,89:1 ✅ Steps to reproduce Go to admin/appearance/settings/default_admin and ensure that the navigation module is installed and then switch between light and dark mode and take a look at the toggle button in the bottom left corner of the screen. Proposed resolution Ensure that the color contrast for the border of the toggle button is at least 3:1 in light mode same as in dark mode. Remaining tasks User interface changes Introduced terminology API changes Data model changes Release notes snippet

No issue in this report was reproduced against an installed Drupal 11.4.12 site in this phase. No screen-reader, keyboard, zoom or forced-colors testing of the assessed release is claimed.

Method and limitations

  1. Paged the complete Drupal Core Accessibility queue (taxonomy term 1101) and kept only active review-relevant statuses: Active, Needs review, Needs work, RTBC and Patch. Fixed and closed issues are excluded.
  2. For a review-priority subset, retrieved the issue, its public comments and every merge request state through drupalorg-cli JSON output. Merged and closed MRs are included. The remaining active issues are complete inventory records (source, status, tags, component, version) with comments, MRs and commit history marked as not collected.
  3. Built a commit index for the target release branch and adjacent development branches, matched commit messages to issue IDs, and checked whether each commit is contained in the release tag using the public GitLab API.
  4. Applied documented classification rules. A tracker report is not a reproduction; commit inclusion is not behavioural verification; a closed issue is not proof of a release fix.
  5. Did not use AI, did not infer conformance and did not assign a percentage or score.

Discovery and tag resolution use the public Drupal.org API. Git containment uses git.drupalcode.org. The in-scope configuration intentionally enables the Default Admin theme; the standard profile in Drupal 11.4.12 uses Claro and Olivero, so this is a declared configuration rather than the stock default.

Limitations

  • The report covers active review-relevant issues carrying the Accessibility tag; it is not a census of every barrier. Closed and fixed issues are excluded by policy. Some active issues are metadata-only inventory records; their commit/comment evidence was not collected in this snapshot.
  • No issue was reproduced against an installed release in this phase. No screen-reader, keyboard, zoom, reduced-motion or forced-colors testing of the assessed release is claimed.
  • Commit matching relies on issue numbers in commit messages. Commits that omit the issue number, or fixes delivered by configuration or contributed modules, are not matched.
  • Commit containment is code inclusion, not proof that the barrier is behaviourally corrected.
  • WCAG criterion tags are candidate relevance signals, not conformance determinations.
  • An issue may describe a barrier that is fixed by a later release or by a downstream distribution; this report does not transfer those conclusions.

The OpenACR file is a schema- and catalog-validated draft skeleton. It intentionally contains no conformance values and uses a placeholder reviewer; a human must populate and approve it before publication. See openacr-validation.json for the check result.
